POWERING INDIA'S NEXT DECADE
Goodluck india limited
Annual Report 2025-26

ACROSS THE PAGES
India is entering a decade defined by scale — in 
energy, in national security, in the infrastructure 
that moves people and goods, and in the precision 
engineering that underpins it all. As the nation 
builds toward 500+ GW of renewable capacity, 
deeper defence self-reliance, and the next 
generation of high-speed rail and transmission 
networks, it needs partners who can manufacture 
at the intersection of steel, technology and trust.
Goodluck India has spent 39 years becoming exactly that 
partner — evolving from a conventional steel products 
company into a diversified engineering solutions 
enterprise built for this moment. FY26 marked the year 
this transformation became measurable: a dedicated 
defence manufacturing facility, growing solar structure 
volumes, a marquee high-speed rail mandate, and a 
product mix increasingly weighted toward high-value, 
specialised engineering.

The Company is positioning itself to play a meaningful 
role in this journey. From its roots in steel products, 
the Company has steadily evolved into a diversified 
engineering solutions provider, expanding its 
capabilities across precision engineering, renewable 
energy, railways, infrastructure, defence, heavy 
fabrication and other specialised applications.
Its growing portfolio of value-added and application-
specific products reflects a deliberate move up the 
value chain, while investments in manufacturing 
capabilities and new businesses are creating 
platforms for future growth. The Company’s 
expansion into hydraulic tubes, solar structures, 
defence manufacturing, high-speed railway projects, 
front fork tubes and GI conduit pipes demonstrates its 
ability to align its capabilities with emerging national 
and global opportunities.
As India builds the foundations for its next decade of 
growth, Goodluck India is engineering the products, 
capabilities and solutions that can help power this 
transformation—strengthening infrastructure, 
enabling new industries and contributing to a more 
self-reliant India.

OUR JOURNEY
COMPANY OVERVIEWSTATUTORY REPORTFINANCIAL STATEMENTS
Annual Report 2025-26 Goodluck India Limited
6 7
[page 6]
Goodluck India Limited has come a long way from where it started 39+ years ago. What 
began in 1986 as a steel manufacturing business, founded by first-generation techpreneur 
Mr. Mahesh Chandra Garg (an IIT Roorkee alumnus), has today grown into a diversified 
engineering solutions company — with three generations of the founding family still hands-
on in the business, backed by a strong professional management team.
The Company works across four core areas — Engineering Structures & Precision Fabrication, Forgings, Precision Pipes 
& Auto Tubes, and CR Coils, Pipes & Tubes — and has recently added a fast-growing Defence & Aerospace vertical through 
its subsidiary, Goodluck Defence & Aerospace Ltd. Its products go into some of the sectors shaping India’s growth story — 
autos, solar, railways, defence, oil & gas and infrastructure — and reach 600+ customers in 100+ countries.

OUR ENGINES 
OF GROWTH
BUSINESS SEGMENTS
Goodluck India Limited operates across four principal, synergistic business 
segments spanning the steel-to-engineering value chain. Over the years, 
the Company has evolved from a conventional steel products manufacturer 
into a diversified engineering solutions provider, with an increasing share 
of revenue coming from high-margin, value-added products catering to 
high-growth sectors such as Automobile, Solar, Railways and Defence.

ENGINEERING STRUCTURE 
& PRECISION FABRICATION
This segment embodies Goodluck’s transformation from a steel products manufacturer into a full-fledged 
engineering solutions provider. From launching girders for high-speed rail corridors to precision structures that 
anchor solar installations and power-generation equipment, the Company designs, fabricates and delivers mission-
critical infrastructure for some of India’s most ambitious railway, energy and renewable programmes — making this 
one of the fastest-growing and most strategically significant segments in the portfolio.

This segment’s growth is anchored in India’s next 
generation of infrastructure investment, particularly 
the high-speed rail programme. New areas of 
opportunity include special formwork for elevated 
corridors and for tunnel boring machines used in 
high-speed rail construction, station buildings and 
super-critical bridges for the bullet train corridor, 
and structures for emerging smart city projects. 
The Company is also expanding into car ports and 
solar parks, offering design engineering, supply and 
architectural structures for wire-drawn bridges, 
broadening its footprint in the renewable energy and 
urban infrastructure space.

FORGINGS
Goodluck’s forging business brings decades of process discipline to some of the most demanding applications in 
industry — from critical defence and aerospace components to flanges and shafts that must perform flawlessly 
in energy and general engineering environments. With a strategic shift towards stainless steel, duplex and alloy 
grades, and a single-piece forging capability now scaled to 14,000 kg, the segment is evolving from a components 
supplier into a trusted end-to-end forging solutions partner for its marquee customers.

    Strategic focus on stainless steel, duplex, carbon 
and alloy steel forgings and flanges, moving the 
Company towards being an end-to-end forged-
solutions provider rather than a component 
supplier alone.
    Well positioned to benefit from anticipated 
recovery in global oil & gas infrastructure 
investment and repair demand, with the Company’s 
forging unit ready to service urgent supply 
requirements.
    Total forging capacity per single piece was 
enhanced to 14,000 kg, supporting larger and more 
complex forged components for defence, energy 
and general engineering applications.
Growth in the forging segment is being driven 
by a decisive shift towards higher-specification 
materials, with an expanding portfolio of stainless 
steel, duplex, carbon and alloy steel forgings and 
flanges catering to increasingly stringent customer 
requirements. The Company is also transforming 
its value proposition from being a manufacturer of 
individual forged components to a provider of end-
to-end forging solutions, enabling deeper customer 
engagement and improved capture of value across 
the product lifecycle.

PRECISION PIPES & AUTO TUBES
Built on decades of metallurgical expertise, this segment manufactures precision-engineered CDW, ERW, 
engineering and boiler tubes that meet the exacting tolerances demanded by the world’s leading automotive and 
engineering brands. As global OEMs increasingly seek lightweight, high-strength tubular components, Goodluck’s 
investments in specialised capacity — including its hydraulic tubes facility — are positioning this segment as one of 
the Company’s fastest-scaling, highest-margin businesses and a cornerstone of its export franchise.

    High-margin, precision-engineered segment that

    The Company’s dedicated hydraulic tubes plant 
(50,000 MTPA capacity), commissioned in FY25 
to substitute imported seamless tubes for

utilisation in FY26 and is targeted to scale to

Precision Tubes division to capture rising demand 
from the automobile and construction equipment 
sectors.
The segment’s growth agenda centres on deepening its 
value-added, export-oriented positioning. Consistent, 
quality-certified deliveries to both domestic and 
international customers have translated into a growing 
share of repeat orders, reflecting sustained customer 
loyalty and confidence in the Company’s engineering 
capabilities. This is reinforced by a deliberate focus on 
high-margin products, which positions the segment as 
one of the Company’s principal contributors to export 
revenue as global OEMs increasingly look to India for 
reliable, precision-engineered tubular components.

COLD FORMED PROFILE, CR 
COILS PIPES & TUBES
The foundation of Goodluck’s manufacturing base, this segment supplies cold rolled coils, sheets and pipes that 
underpin the Company’s largest installed capacity and its longest-standing customer relationships. Serving 
exclusively OEMs and government infrastructure programmes, the segment combines scale with consistency — 
and is now being augmented with higher-value, export-oriented products such as GI conduit pipes, sharpening its 
margin profile even as it continues to anchor the Company’s overall volumes.

20 21
[page 13]
Scale, on its own, is not an achievement. What Goodluck has built over FY24–FY26 is scale 
with discipline — capacity added precisely where demand justified it, utilisation pushed 
higher even as the base grew larger, and a quality system rigorous enough to certify 
components for defence and aerospace without ever leaving the building. 
The facility is licensed to manufacture artillery shells across all 
major calibres — 105mm, 120mm, 125mm, 130mm and 155mm — 
including variants such as HE M107, ERFB, ERFB BB and ERFB 
BT. That breadth matters: it positions the Company to serve 
multiple platforms and customer specifications from a single 
facility, rather than being locked into one calibre and one buyer.
R&D and Quality: The NABL-Accredited Testing Lab
Manufacturing at scale is only as credible as the testing standing behind it. The Company runs its own in-house R&D 
set-up, anchored by a NABL (ISO/IEC 17025) accredited Quality Testing Lab — a distinction relatively few Indian steel and 
engineering manufacturers hold in-house, most preferring to outsource it entirely.
That distinction shows up in what the Company can actually do: certify precision components for automotive, aerospace, 
defence and nuclear-adjacent infrastructure without waiting on an external lab’s queue or sign-off. Paired with company-
wide ISO 9001:2008 certification, it forms the quality spine running through every value-added product line — from front 
fork tubes to artillery shell components.
Why This Facility Matters
Very few Indian companies 
hold a manufacturing 
license of this kind. It 
places Goodluck in a select 
group of licensed private 
defence manufacturers, 
aligned with the national 
push toward Atmanirbhar 
Bharat in ammunition 
production — and gives the 
Company a foothold in a 
category with structurally 
limited domestic supply 
relative to demand.

At Goodluck India, innovation is reflected in our continuous transition from conventional 
steel manufacturing to high-value, technology-driven and application-specific engineering 
solutions. Our focus is on developing capabilities, products and processes that address 
evolving requirements across infrastructure, renewable energy, defence, railways, precision 
engineering and other critical industries.

What’s Already in Place
The Company’s NABL-accredited in-house testing 
infrastructure already works as a form of quality 
digitalisation — enabling faster, internally verifiable 
certification cycles for precision components, 
instead of waiting on external labs and their 
turnaround queues.
What Comes Next
As capacity keeps growing across hydraulic tubes, 
defence, and precision tube lines, the obvious 
next step is bringing that same rigour to the 
production line itself — real-time monitoring and 
digital traceability — something worth spelling out 
in more detail in future disclosures as specific 
initiatives take shape.

BUILDING CAPABILITIES FOR THE  
INDUSTRIES SHAPING TOMORROW
INNOV ATION IN ACTION
DIGITALISATION AND INDUSTRY 4.0
Steel manufacturing has a reputation for being a low-tech, heavy-industry business — but the reality on Goodluck’s shop 
floors is shifting. As the Company adds precision-heavy product lines like hydraulic tubes and artillery shells, the tolerance 
for error shrinks, and so does the room for manual, paper-based quality checks. That’s pushing quality and testing 
processes to become more systematic and more digitally verifiable, even where a full factory-wide automation programme 
hasn’t yet been rolled out and formally disclosed.
FROM STEEL TO SOLUTIONS | INNOV ATION JOURNEY
Capacity hasn’t just grown — it’s grown unevenly, and that unevenness is the story. The Company chose to concentrate 
expansion in its higher-value segments rather than scale everything at the same pace.

Dear Shareholders,
It gives me great pleasure 
to present to you the Annual 
Report of Goodluck India 
Limited for the financial 
year 2025-26 — a year that 
I regard as one of the most 
defining chapters in our 
Company’s 39-year journey. 
What began decades ago as 
a conventional steel product 
manufacturer has, through 
deliberate and disciplined 
transformation, evolved into 
a diversified engineering 
solutions enterprise with 
a growing footprint across 
infrastructure, renewable 
energy, defence, railways, oil 
& gas, precision engineering 
and heavy fabrication.
A YEAR OF STRUCTURAL TRANSFORMATION
The year under review tested the resilience of businesses across the world. Volatile steel prices, elevated input costs, 
geopolitical uncertainty and shifting demand patterns created a challenging operating backdrop. I am proud to say that 
your Company not only navigated these headwinds but used them to sharpen its strategic focus — steadily moving up the 
value chain, deepening its presence in high-margin, technology-driven businesses, and reinforcing the foundations of a 
more resilient, sustainable enterprise.
Improving earnings quality. 
Our consolidated EBITDA grew 
significantly faster than revenue 
during the year, rising 26% even 
as total income grew a modest 
4.1%. This operating leverage is 
the clearest evidence yet that 
our shift towards value-added, 
technology-driven products — rather 
than commoditised steel — is 
translating into tangible earnings 
quality. Standalone EBITDA margin 
expanded from 8.4% to 10.2%, and 
consolidated PAT margin improved 
to 4.4%, both reflecting the 
benefits of a richer product mix and 
disciplined operational execution.
Entering the defence value chain. 
One of the most significant 
milestones of the year was the 
commissioning of our dedicated 
defence manufacturing facility 
under our wholly-owned subsidiary, 
Goodluck Defence & Aerospace 
Limited, in October 2025. Having 
received an Industrial License 
under the Indian Arms Act, 1959 
for manufacturing artillery shells 
across all major calibres, we now 
stand among a select league of 
licensed defence manufacturers 
in India. With an initial capacity 
of 1,50,000 shells per annum — 
being scaled up to 4,00,000 shells 
per annum — and an investment 
outlay of approximately ₹500 Cr, 
this vertical positions Goodluck 
to participate meaningfully in the 
global re-armament cycle and 
India’s Atmanirbhar Bharat vision for 
defence self-reliance.
Widening our engineering footprint. 
Beyond defence, we continued 
to strengthen our presence in 
renewable energy, with solar 
structure volumes growing by 
approximately 33% during the 
year, and in infrastructure, where 
our engineering structures and 
precision fabrication business 
— including a marquee Letter of 
Intent from L&T worth over USD 
27 million for India’s High-Speed 
Rail programme — reaffirms our 
role as a trusted partner in nation-
building projects. Our new hydraulic 
tubes facility, aimed at import 
substitution, and our upcoming 
capacities in GI conduit pipes and 
front fork tubes further diversify our 
high-margin product basket.

OPPORTUNITIES
BUILDING INDIA’S ENGINEERING BACKBONE — 
DEFENCE, RENEW ABLE ENERGY, RAILW AYS AND 
INFRASTRUCTURE
Over the past few years, Goodluck India Limited has consciously transformed itself from a conventional steel product 
manufacturer into a diversified engineering solutions player, with growing focus on high-value, technology-driven and 
application-specific businesses. Today, the Company’s presence spans infrastructure, renewable energy, defence, railways, 
oil & gas, precision engineering, heavy fabrication and industrial applications — a diversification that is building a stronger 
and more resilient business model with improved earnings quality and long-term sustainability.
THE OPPORTUNITY
Defence has emerged as one of the world’s most compelling long-term growth sectors, driven by rising geopolitical 
tensions, increasing military modernisation and a global push towards self-reliance in defence manufacturing. In India, 
favourable government policies, higher defence spending and growing private-sector participation are creating a strong 
platform for domestic manufacturers to capitalise on this structural opportunity.
Commercial production at the 
defence facility commenced in FY 
2025-26, following the receipt of the 
manufacturing licence in October 
2025. Consequently, the exceptionally 
high FY26 EBITDA margin reflects 
a limited operating period against 
depreciation and finance costs 
incurred over a longer commissioning 
phase. As operations stabilise and 
volumes scale, management continues 
to maintain a sustainable EBITDA 
margin guidance of 30–35% for the 
defence business.
Beyond artillery shells, Goodluck 
has established a strong presence 
across India’s defence ecosystem 
through its Forgings and Engineering 
Structures businesses, supplying 
critical components to leading defence 
institutions, OEMs and strategic 
national programmes.
Key Associations
• Government & PSU Customers: HAL, 
BEML, Mazagon Dock Shipbuilders, 
Ordnance Factory Board (Avadi & 
Medak), ISRO and DRDO.
• OEM & Tier-1 Customers: BrahMos 
Aerospace, L&T Defence and Godrej 
Aerospace.
• Strategic Programmes: Pralay 
Missiles, HAL HTFE Programme, 
BrahMos Missiles, Pinaka Rockets, 
K9 Vajra Howitzer and Talwar-class 
Frigates.
Sources: PIB — Defence in Union Budget 2026–27; Business Standard — FY26 arms production hits record ₹1.78 trn
Key Opportunity Drivers
• Record global defence spending: 
Military expenditure has reached 
approximately US$2.89 trillion, 
representing nearly 2.5% of global 
GDP, reflecting sustained investment 
in defence capabilities.
• Global rearmament cycle: Initiatives 
such as ReArm Europe, ReArm Gulf 
and the U.S. Replenish programme 
are expected to drive multi-year 
defence procurement across major 
economies.
• India’s defence indigenisation: 
Under the Atmanirbhar Bharat 
initiative, private-sector 
participation in defence 
manufacturing is expected to 
increase from around 30% to 
nearly 50%, creating significant 
opportunities for domestic players.
• Higher government spending: The 
Union Budget FY27 allocated ₹7.85 
lakh crore to defence, including ₹1.85 
lakh crore for capital acquisition, 
with approximately 75% reserved for 
domestic procurement.
• Strong industry momentum: 
India’s domestic defence production 
reached a record ₹1.78 lakh crore 
in FY26, up 16% YoY, while defence 
exports touched an all-time high 
of ₹38,424 crore, underscoring 
the sector’s accelerating growth 
trajectory.

THE OPPORTUNITY
India’s renewable energy transition continues to gain momentum, supported by record capacity additions and ambitious 
clean energy targets. As solar installations accelerate, demand for engineered solar structures and transmission 
infrastructure is expected to rise significantly, creating long-term opportunities for companies with specialised 
manufacturing capabilities such as Goodluck India.
GOODLUCK’S POSITION — THE NUMBERS
Sources: PIB — India ranks third globally in renewable energy capacity; Swarajya — India adds record power capacity in FY26, crosses 520 GW
Key Opportunity Drivers
• Rapid capacity expansion: India’s 
total installed power generation 
capacity surpassed 520 GW in FY26, 
with renewable energy accounting 
for the majority of new capacity 
additions.
• Solar growth momentum: Installed 
solar capacity reached 150.26 GW as 
of March 2026, reflecting more than 
53-fold growth since 2014.
• Strong policy support: India’s non-
fossil energy capacity has crossed 
300 GW, progressing towards the 
Government’s 500 GW target by 2030.
• Growing demand for engineered 
structures: Every gigawatt of 
new solar capacity requires 
specialised mounting structures, 
tracking systems and transmission 
infrastructure, supporting sustained 
demand for Goodluck’s engineered 
steel solutions.
• Transmission infrastructure 
opportunity: The rapid expansion 
of solar generation is outpacing 
transmission capacity, creating 
additional opportunities for 
Goodluck’s Engineering Structures 
and Transmission Tubes businesses.

THE OPPORTUNITY
India’s railway modernisation programme — spanning high-speed rail (the “bullet train” corridor), the PM GatiShakti 
multimodal infrastructure push, and metro/urban rail expansion — is generating sustained demand for specialised 
steel structures: station buildings, super-critical and special bridges, tunnel-boring-machine formwork, girders and 
transmission/signal structures. Chairman M.C. Garg described infrastructure and high-speed rail as “the backbone of every 
transformation,” noting that GatiShakti is “envisaging further high-speed trains, where your company is a major player in 
terms of this transmission.”
This modernisation is backed by substantial capital commitment. Indian Railways’ gross budgetary support for capex stood 
at ₹2.52 lakh crore in FY26, augmented by ₹10,000 crore of extra-budgetary resources for PPP projects, while the 2026-27 
Union Budget allocated roughly ₹16 lakh crore collectively toward seven proposed high-speed rail corridors. On the flagship 
Mumbai–Ahmedabad High-Speed Rail corridor (508 km, 320 kmph design speed, 12 stations), the viaduct is now about 90% 
elevated and the first service is targeted for August 2027 — the scale of build programme that plays directly to Goodluck’s 
special-bridge and precision-tube capabilities.

THE OPPORTUNITY
Infrastructure development continues to be one of India’s strongest long-term growth drivers, supported by sustained 
government investments, industrial expansion and global rebuilding activities. Rising investments across construction, 
oil & gas and industrial infrastructure are expected to generate significant demand for value-added steel products, 
engineering structures and precision components, positioning Goodluck India to capitalise on these structural growth 
opportunities.
The Company maintains a healthy order book across all business segments, providing strong revenue visibility. As of March 
2026, net debt stood at approximately ₹1,000 crore, against cash and bank balances of around ₹50 crore. While continuing 
to invest in strategic capacity expansion, the Company remains committed to a structured debt repayment plan, with 
scheduled repayments of ₹54 crore in FY27 and reinforcing its focus on disciplined capital management alongside long-
term growth.
Sources: IBEF — Infrastructure Development in India; MarkNtel Advisors — India Steel Market Report
Key Opportunity Drivers
• Robust infrastructure pipeline: 
India’s National Infrastructure 
Pipeline (NIP) comprises investments 
of approximately ₹213 lakh crore 
across more than 14,500 projects, 
providing long-term visibility for 
infrastructure-led demand.
• Higher capital expenditure: The 
Government has projected nearly 
₹143 lakh crore of infrastructure 
spending through FY30, while FY27 
capital expenditure stands at ₹12.22 
lakh crore, reinforcing continued 
investment momentum.
• Growing steel demand: India’s steel 
market, valued at around US$150.5 
billion in 2026, is projected to grow 
at a 7.1% CAGR through 2032, with 
the building and construction sector 
accounting for nearly 63% of total 
steel consumption.
• Oil & gas infrastructure opportunity: 
Ongoing rebuilding and replacement 
of oil & gas infrastructure, 
particularly in regions impacted by 
geopolitical disruptions, is expected 
to create additional demand for 
specialised forging products.
• Strategic market alignment: 
Goodluck India’s portfolio of CR 
coils, precision tubes, engineering 
structures and forgings is well 
aligned with the growing demand 
emerging from infrastructure, 
construction and industrial 
development

PEOPLE POWERING PROGRESS
HUMAN RESOURCES
BUILDING CAPABILITY 
FOR TOMORROW
LEARNING & DEVELOPMENT
Building a capable, safe and future-ready workforce to support Goodluck India’s growth.
Continuous learning strengthens technical capability, safety awareness and organisational 
resilience.
At Goodluck India Limited, people are central to the Company’s ability to 
manufacture, innovate and deliver with consistency. As the business continues to 
strengthen its operations, the Company remains focused on building a workforce 
that is skilled, safe, engaged and equipped for the future.
During FY 2025–26, the Company’s workforce stood at 7,413 employees and 
workers, comprising 1,344 permanent employees and 6,069 workers. The Company 
continued to focus on capability development, workplace safety, employee well-
being, fair employment practices and accessible grievance mechanisms.
The Company believes that continuous learning is essential to building a future-ready workforce. During FY 2025–26, 
training focused on health and safety measures, skill upgradation and awareness of human rights and relevant policies.
The Company maintained a strong focus on awareness of human rights. 
Human-rights training covered 1,342 of 1,344 permanent employees and 
5,957 of 6,069 workers during the year, representing coverage of 99.85% 
and 98.15%, respectively.
The Company strengthened its performance and career-development review process during the year. Coverage increased 
materially compared with FY 2024–25, reinforcing a more structured approach to employee and worker development.

Goodluck India seeks to support its workforce through statutory benefits and welfare measures. During FY 2025–26, PF and 
gratuity coverage stood at 100% for employees and workers, with applicable dues deposited with the relevant authorities. 
Employees and workers also have access to non-occupational medical and healthcare services, including general medical 
care, health check-ups and specialist consultations.
The Company is committed to ensuring equal opportunities and maintaining a fair and non-discriminatory work 
environment. Its Equal Opportunity Policy promotes diversity and inclusiveness, with opportunities provided on the basis 
of merit, skills and capabilities. The Company has also ensured accessibility across its premises and offices for differently 
abled employees and workers
As Goodluck India continues to grow, its human-capital priorities will remain focused on building skills, strengthening 
safety, enabling career development and fostering a fair and inclusive workplace. The Company is also planning to 
establish transition-assistance programmes to facilitate continued employability and support career endings arising from 
retirement or termination.

The Company has implemented an Occupational Health and Safety Management 
System covering its operations. Hazard Identification and Risk Assessment 
(HIRA) studies are undertaken across departmental activities to identify routine 
and non-routine hazards, assess associated risks and implement appropriate 
controls. Employees and workers have processes to report work-related 
hazards and unsafe conditions.
The Company has established a structured grievance-management mechanism. A grievance register is placed at 
the entrance gate, enabling staff to record concerns during working hours. The Grievance Redressal Committee is 
committed to addressing grievances within 48 hours, and committee members’ names and contact details are displayed 
on notice boards.

GOVERNANCE
At Goodluck India, strong governance is integral to building a resilient and sustainable 
business. Our governance framework is anchored in accountability, transparency, ethical 
conduct and responsible decision-making, enabling effective oversight as the Company 
expands into high-value and strategically important sectors. As Goodluck India continues its 
transformation into a diversified engineering solutions provider, the Board and leadership 
remain focused on maintaining robust governance practices, prudent financial discipline 
and a clear alignment between business priorities and stakeholder interests.

As the Company expands its portfolio and enters increasingly specialised sectors, governance assumes an important

MANAGEMENT DISCUSSION & ANALYSIS 
Global Economic Environment
The global economy continued to demonstrate resilience amid 
a period of economic and geopolitical transition. Moderating 
inflation, healthy labour market conditions and improving 
liquidity conditions supported growth across many regions. 
Emerging economies remained important contributors to 
Regional Growth Trends
Global output is estimated to have expanded by 3.4% in 
2025, marginally higher than the 3.3% recorded in 2024. The 
increase was largely supported by resilient domestic demand 
in advanced economies, offsetting the moderation witnessed 
in certain large emerging markets. Growth is projected to 
ease to 3.1% in 2026 before improving slightly to 3.2% in 2027, 
reflecting the lingering effects of elevated interest rates, 
softer industrial activity and slower trade growth.
Within advanced economies, growth improved modestly 
to 1.9% in 2025 from 1.7% in the previous year. Economic 
performance, however, varied across regions. The United 
States witnessed a moderation in growth as higher borrowing 
costs tempered consumer and business spending, while the 
The global economy maintained a steady growth trajectory during FY 2025–26, supported by easing inflationary 
pressures, resilient consumer demand and improving financial conditions across several major markets. While 
geopolitical tensions, shifting trade relationships and supply chain realignments continued to shape the operating 
environment, economic activity remained relatively stable. Policymakers across advanced and emerging economies 
focused on balancing growth and price stability, helping sustain business confidence and investment activity despite 
an evolving global landscape.

Euro Area benefited from easing energy costs and a gradual 
industrial recovery. Going forward, growth in advanced 
economies is expected to remain stable, supported by 
improving financial conditions and gradually easing monetary 
policies.
Emerging and developing economies continued to account 
for the majority of global growth. Emerging and Developing 
Asia remained the fastest-growing region, driven by strong 
domestic demand and ongoing investment activity. India 
continued to outperform major economies and is projected 
to remain among the fastest-growing large economies 
globally. Meanwhile, Sub-Saharan Africa maintained steady 
momentum, supported by structural reforms, infrastructure 
investments and favourable demographic trends.

Outlook
The global economy is expected to expand at a moderate pace over the medium term, although growth rates are likely to remain 
below historical averages. Key uncertainties include geopolitical developments, evolving trade policies, regional conflicts and 
increasing fragmentation of global supply chains. These factors may continue to influence investment decisions, capital flows 
and international trade patterns.
Nevertheless, the gradual easing of monetary policy across several advanced economies, coupled with improving inflation 
dynamics and stabilising financial markets, is expected to provide support to global economic activity. Continued progress in 
international trade relations, stable commodity markets and contained geopolitical disruptions will remain important factors in 
sustaining global growth and fostering a more predictable business environment.

INDIAN ECONOMY
India further strengthened its position as a key driver of global economic growth during FY 2025–26, emerging as the world’s 
fourth-largest economy in nominal GDP terms. This achievement underscores the effectiveness of sustained policy reforms, 
robust infrastructure creation, a stable macroeconomic environment and the strength of domestic consumption. Supported 
by favourable demographics, increasing formalisation and a growing investment ecosystem, India remains well-positioned to 
enhance its economic prominence on the global stage.
The economy expanded at a real GDP growth rate of 7.6% during FY 2025–26, compared with 7.1% in the previous year, while 
nominal GDP rose by 8.6% to `345.47 lakh crore. Growth momentum was underpinned by resilient consumer spending, sustained 
government capital expenditure, rising private investments and healthy activity across manufacturing and services sectors. The 
breadth of this expansion reflects the underlying strength, adaptability and long-term potential of the Indian economy.
Sectoral Performance
India’s growth trajectory during FY 2025–26 was supported by 
strong performance across both the industrial and services 
sectors, reflecting the breadth and resilience of the economy. 
The industrial sector registered a growth rate of 8.8%, driven 
by sustained momentum in manufacturing, infrastructure 
development and construction activity. Manufacturing 
emerged as a key contributor to economic expansion, 
supported by capacity additions, increasing investments and 
policy initiatives aimed at enhancing domestic production 
and strengthening industrial competitiveness.
Outlook
India’s strong economic fundamentals, sustained infrastructure investments and growing emphasis on manufacturing-led 
development are expected to create significant opportunities for the steel and engineering products industry. Government 
initiatives such as the National Infrastructure Pipeline (NIP), PM Gati Shakti, Dedicated Freight Corridors, expansion of renewable 
energy capacity and continued investments in railways, transmission networks and industrial infrastructure are expected to drive 
long-term demand for steel-intensive products and engineering solutions.
The increasing focus on domestic manufacturing, import substitution and strategic sectors such as defence, renewable energy 
and transportation is expected to further strengthen demand for value-added steel products. At the same time, rising private 
sector capital expenditure and improving industrial activity are likely to support growth across key end-user industries.

The services sector remained the largest contributor to 
economic output, recording a growth rate of 9.3% during the 
year, up from 7.9% in FY 2024–25. The trade, transport and 
communication segment led the sector’s performance with 
growth of 10.1%, reflecting improving business activity, rising 
mobility and strengthening consumption trends. Growth was 
further supported by robust services exports, expanding 
employment opportunities and increasing urban demand. The 
continued expansion of the services ecosystem is expected to 
support economic activity across infrastructure, real estate 
and other consumption-oriented sectors, reinforcing India’s 
long -term growth outlook.
Source: MoSPI, Provisional Estimates of Annual GDP 2025–26. 
Inflation and Monetary Environment
India’s macroeconomic landscape remained favourable during FY 2025–26, underpinned by controlled inflation and a supportive 
monetary policy framework. Consumer price inflation remained comfortably within the Reserve Bank of India’s tolerance 
band, aided by moderation in food and energy prices. The stable inflationary environment helped preserve purchasing power, 
strengthened consumer sentiment and contributed to overall economic stability.
The moderation in price pressures created room for accommodative monetary measures aimed at sustaining growth momentum. 
During the year, the Reserve Bank of India reduced the repo rate to 5.25% and lowered the Cash Reserve Ratio (CRR) to 3.0%, 
supporting liquidity conditions and improving the flow of credit across the financial system.
A combination of stable prices, lower borrowing costs and enhanced liquidity has created a conducive environment for investment 
and consumption. Improved access to capital for businesses and households is expected to support economic activity, strengthen 
demand and foster long-term growth across sectors, while also enhancing the overall operating environment for enterprises.
Source: PIB Press Release PRID 2219907; RBI Monetary Policy Committee Statement
Source: NSO CPI Press Release, April 2026; RBI Monetary Policy Statements.
With its diversified product portfolio, integrated 
manufacturing facilities and established presence across 
infrastructure, railways, renewable energy, automotive, oil 
& gas and defence sectors, Goodluck India Limited is well 
positioned to capitalise on these emerging opportunities. 
The Company’s continued focus on value-added products, 
operational excellence, technology upgradation and 
capacity expansion, including its growing presence in the 
defence segment, is expected to strengthen its competitive 
positioning and support sustainable long-term growth.

INDUSTRY OVERVIEW
GLOBAL STEEL INDUSTRY
Total world crude steel production stood at 1,849.4 Mt in 
2025, with China alone accounting for approximately 961 Mt — 
roughly 52% of the global total. However, output is softening 
in early 2026: global crude steel production was 153.4 Mt 
in April 2026, a 1.9% decrease year-on-year, with Asia and 
Oceania down 1.3%, the Middle East sharply down 27.6%, 
and Russia & CIS down 13.4% — partially offset by North 
On the supply side, the OECD warns that global steelmaking capacity is set to keep rising through 2028 even amid sluggish 
demand, threatening to depress capacity utilisation and squeeze profitability further, with competition increasingly distorted by 
subsidies concentrated outside the OECD area. Geopolitically, the Middle East conflict has sharply reversed regional demand, and 
if hostilities persist beyond Q2 2026, material downward revisions to global forecasts would become necessary.
Sources: worldsteel Short Range Outlook, April 2026 | worldsteel April 2026 Crude Steel Production | OECD Steel Outlook 2026
INDIA STEEL INDUSTRY
India is the world's second-largest steel producer and consumer, with its share of global crude steel production rising from 5.2% 
in 2014 to 7.9% in 2024. Driven by rapid infrastructure development, urbanisation, and manufacturing growth, both production and 
consumption have more than doubled over the past decade.
Production & Consumption (MT)
Iron & Intermediate Products (Apr–Sep)
On trade, exports of finished steel surged 35.8% in FY 2025–26 while imports fell 46.47%, with Vietnam, Belgium and Taiwan 
together accounting for over 50% of India's finished steel exports in March 2026. On the policy front, the PLI scheme has mobilised 
`23,022 crore in investment, created 13,264 direct jobs, and a third round (PLI 1.2) covering 85 projects worth `11,887 crore was 
launched in November 2025. India targets 500 MT of steel production capacity by 2047. On green steel, India introduced the world's 
first Green Steel Taxonomy in 2024; as of March 2026, 89 units are certified covering 12.34 MT, backed by `20,000 crore allocated 
for CCUS in Budget 2026–27 and a net-zero emission intensity target by 2070.
Source: Ministry of Steel, PIB — May 2026
America rising 6.9% and Africa up 11.5%. On the demand side, 
worldsteel forecasts global steel demand to grow 0.3% in 
2026 to 1,724 Mt, accelerating to 2.2% in 2027 at 1,762 Mt, as 
the market bottoms out after years of structural adjustment. 
India leads growth at +7.4% in 2026, while China's contraction 
narrows to -1.5%.

GLOBAL METAL FORGING MARKET
The global metal forging market was valued at USD 70.36 
billion in 2025 and is projected to grow from USD 73.43 billion 
in 2026 to USD 103.35 billion by 2034, at a CAGR of 4.40%. 
Asia Pacific dominated with a 44.90% market share in 2025, 
led by rapid industrialisation, infrastructure development, 
and strong automotive and heavy machinery demand across 
China, India, Japan, and Southeast Asia.

INDIA METAL FORGING MARKET
The Indian metal forging industry continues to exhibit 
strong growth momentum, driven by increasing demand 
from automotive, railways, defense, renewable energy, 
infrastructure, and industrial machinery sectors. As one of 
the world's leading forging hubs, India benefits from a robust 
manufacturing ecosystem, cost competitiveness, skilled 
workforce, and growing integration into global supply chains. 
According to industry estimates, the Indian metal forging 
market was valued at approximately USD 4.2 billion in 2024 
and is projected to grow at a CAGR of around 7% during 2025–
2034. Growth is being supported by rising infrastructure 
investments, increasing localization of manufacturing, 
expanding defense production, and growing exports of 
engineering products.
The automotive sector remains the largest consumer of 
forged components, while emerging opportunities in defense, 
railways, renewable energy, and capital goods are further 
strengthening industry demand. Additionally, government 
initiatives such as "Make in India" and "Atmanirbhar Bharat" are 
accelerating domestic manufacturing and enhancing India's 
position as a preferred global sourcing destination for high-
quality forged products.
With continued investments in technology, precision 
engineering, and value-added manufacturing, the Indian 
forging industry remains well-positioned to capitalize 
on the country's long-term industrial and economic  
growth prospects.

GLOBAL AUTOMOBILE INDUSTRY
The global automotive sector crossed a significant milestone in 2025, with new light vehicle sales rising 3.4% to approximately 91.7 
million units — surpassing pre-pandemic highs for the first time. The global automotive market is valued at USD 2,328.5 billion in 
2026 and is projected to reach USD 2,982.8 billion by 2035, expanding at a CAGR of 2.79% over the forecast period. This sustained 
growth is driven by rising per capita incomes, rapid EV adoption, and the expanding role of advanced driver assistance systems 
(ADAS) and autonomous vehicle technologies across key markets.

Electrification: The Defining Shift
Global EV sales reached 20.7 million units in 2025 — over a quarter of all new cars sold globally — growing 20% year-on-year. 
The rapid adoption of electric vehicles continues to be a primary growth engine for the automotive market, offering superior 
fuel efficiency, lower maintenance costs, and reduced environmental impact compared to internal combustion engine vehicles. 
China led with EVs comprising nearly 47.9% of its domestic market. The EU’s battery-electric share rose to 17.4%, while the 
U.S. registered its highest-ever quarterly EV sales in Q3 2025. For 2026, global EV volumes are forecast at approximately 22.7 
million units, representing ~24.7% of the market. Major OEMs have collectively committed over USD 500 billion in EV production 
investments by 2030, cementing the industry’s structural shift toward zero-emission mobility.
show sizable growth, driven by the accelerating adoption of

Looking ahead, global light vehicle sales in 2026 are projected 
to remain near-flat at around 91.8 million units, as tariff 
impacts, high interest rates, and uneven EV adoption temper 
near-term momentum. However, the long-term structural 
outlook remains positive, underpinned by the dual tailwinds of 
electrification and autonomous mobility.
Autonomous Vehicles: The Next Frontier
The rising prominence of autonomous and semi-autonomous 
vehicles represents another significant long-term driver 
for the automotive market. Leveraging AI, LiDAR, RADAR, 
and advanced camera systems, autonomous vehicles are 
expected to enhance road safety, improve fuel efficiency, and 
reduce traffic congestion. As regulatory frameworks mature 
and technology costs decline, autonomous vehicle adoption 
is projected to accelerate meaningfully through 2030 and 
beyond.

INDIAN AUTOMOBILE INDUSTRY
India’s automobile industry closed FY 2025–26 on a historic 
high, with every major vehicle category — passenger vehicles, 
commercial vehicles, three-wheelers, and two-wheelers — 
posting its all-time highest annual sales. This marks the first 
industry-wide record sweep since FY 2018–19. Total domestic 
wholesale volumes crossed 2.83 crore units, propelled by 
GST 2.0 reforms, successive RBI repo rate cuts, and stronger 
purchasing power from personal income tax relief. The year 
followed a distinctive arc: a soft first half (H1: -1.4%) gave 
way to an exceptionally strong second half (H2: +16.7%), 
underscoring the swift impact of policy tailwinds on consumer 
demand.
Passenger Vehicles (PV): PV sales reached a record 46.43 lakh 
units, growing 7.9% year-on-year, with Q4 alone clocking 13.16 
lakh units (+13.2%). Utility Vehicles continued to dominate the 
mix, reflecting sustained consumer preference for SUVs. On 
the export front, PV shipments rose 16% to 8.63 lakh units 
in CY 2025 — the highest ever — with utility vehicle exports 
surging 32%.

segments. EV penetration is projected to reach 9.5–10% in FY

INFRASTRUCTURE SECTOR IN INDIA
Infrastructure remains the backbone of India’s long-term 
growth agenda. The India Infrastructure Market, valued at USD 
190.51 billion in 2025, is projected to reach USD 205.96 billion 
in 2026 and expand to USD 302.62 billion by 2031, registering 
a robust CAGR of 8.0% over the forecast period. This 
sustained momentum is driven by strong policy coordination, 
accelerating private participation, and India’s strategic push 
toward becoming a USD 26 trillion economy by 2047.
The government’s flagship PM Gati Shakti platform — 
overlaying geospatial data across 16 ministries — has materially 
shortened project execution timelines. By December 2025, 
inter-ministerial clearance times for logistics parks were 
halved from 18 to 9 months, with highway projects reaching 
financial closure within 120 days rising to 87%, up from 62% 
two years earlier. These digital-led efficiency gains are 
Looking ahead, India's infrastructure sector is expected to maintain strong growth momentum, supported by continued public 
investment, increasing private sector participation, asset monetization initiatives, and the government's long-term vision of 
creating world-class infrastructure. These developments are expected to generate sustained demand for structural steel, 
engineering products, precision tubes, and value-added steel solutions across multiple end-use sectors.
directly translating into improved bid competitiveness and 
lower interest costs for concessionaires.
The National Green Hydrogen Mission — targeting 5 million 
tonnes of annual capacity by 2030 — is catalysing an 
estimated USD 100 billion of pipeline, port, and storage 
infrastructure. Sovereign green bonds (two tranches totalling 
USD 4 billion) have lowered borrowing costs for metro and EV 
charging projects by 80–100 basis points, while a third USD 3 
billion tranche planned for FY 2026 will fund coastal highway 
climate-proofing. Simultaneously, surging hyperscale data-
centre demand — with India’s installed capacity reaching 1,100 
MW in 2025 and 1,800 MW under construction — is catalysing 
renewable-powered industrial parks in Hyderabad, Mumbai, 
and Pune.

COMPANY OVERVIEWSTATUTORY REPORTFINANCIAL STATEMENTS
India's roads and railways sectors continued to remain at 
the forefront of the country's infrastructure-led growth 
strategy during FY 2025-26. Supported by sustained 
government investments and policy initiatives aimed at 
improving connectivity, logistics efficiency, and economic 
competitiveness, both sectors witnessed significant progress 
in capacity creation and modernization.
India’s road infrastructure programme delivered another 
strong year in FY 2025–26. The National Highways Authority 
of India (NHAI) constructed 5,313 km of national highways — 
surpassing its annual target of 4,640 km by approximately 
15%. While slightly below the 5,614 km built in FY 2024–25, the 
achievement reflects consistent execution capability across 
expressways, economic corridors, and strategic highway 
segments. Capital expenditure by NHAI for FY 2025–26 stood 
at `2,44,362 crore, approximately 2.5% higher than the 
government’s budgetary support for the year.
The total national highway network now spans over 1,46,204 
km, positioning India as home to the world’s second-largest 
road network of over 6.62 million km. The 4-lane and above 
national highway network has more than doubled — from 
18,371 km in 2014 to 43,512 km presently — reflecting the 
transformative impact of decade-long investment in road 
infrastructure. MoRTH constructed 10,660 km of highways in 
FY 2024–25, exceeding its target of 10,421 km, and is targeting 
construction of over 10,000 km in FY 2025–26 with projects 
worth `3 trillion lined up for award.
Key Highlights
Budget Allocation
Under Union Budget 2026–27, the Ministry of Road Transport 
and Highways received an allocation of `1,21,999 crore (USD 
13.6 billion) for roads and bridges — a 5% increase over the 
revised estimates for FY 2025–26.
Asset Monetisation
MoRTH cumulatively monetised `1,52,028 crore through TOT, 
InvIT, and toll securitisation modes up to November 2025, 
targeting `30,000 crore in FY 2025–26 alone. Additionally, 
`9,270 crore was awarded under the TOT mode in FY 2025–26.
Greenfield Corridors
27 greenfield corridors spanning 9,860 km are under active 
development, aligned with the national masterplan for 2047. 
A PPP project pipeline of 13,400 km has been identified to 
attract private investment at scale.
Technology & Innovation
NHAI partnered with IIIT Delhi to deploy AI for road safety 
monitoring. Digital highways with optic fibre cable 
infrastructure along major expressways are being developed 
to enable 5G and 6G connectivity. NHAI was also conferred the 
‘Gold Award’ for its Drone Analytics Monitoring System (DAMS) 
by the Government of India.
India’s road sector continues to be a critical enabler for 
Goodluck’s Engineering Structures and Fabrication vertical, as 
growing demand for highway bridges, girders, and structural 
steel components for expressway and corridor projects drives 
sustained order inflows.
Sources:
• NHAI — FY 2025–26 National Highway Development (News on AIR)
• IBEF — Roads & Highways Sector India

• Construction World — NHAI FY26 Highway Growth

Indian Railways closed FY 2025–26 with a series of historic 
milestones, reinforcing its position as one of the world’s 
largest and most transformative rail networks. The national 
transporter carried approximately 741 crore passengers 
during the year while operating nearly 25,000 trains daily. 
Freight loading crossed a landmark 1 billion tonnes in FY 
2025–26 and reached a record 1,670 million tonnes — keeping 
Indian Railways firmly on its trajectory to become the world’s 
second-largest goods-carrying railway after China.
Capital expenditure for FY 2025–26 stood at `2,52,000 crore 
(budgetary support), with the Railways having utilised over 
80% of gross budgetary support within the first nine months 
of the fiscal year — a 6.54 percentage point improvement 
over the prior year. Union Budget 2026–27 has further 
raised the capital outlay to a record `2,78,000 crore (USD 
29.94 billion), with provisions for seven new high-speed rail 
corridors, underscoring sustained policy commitment to rail 
infrastructure.
Key Highlights
Electrification
Indian Railways achieved 99.6% electrification of the broad-
gauge network in FY 2025–26 — a transformational shift from 
near-zero in 2014. This has significantly reduced dependence 
on imported crude oil, lowered carbon emissions, and 
enhanced operational efficiency across the network.
Roads & Railways
Vande Bharat & Fleet Modernisation
164 Vande Bharat Express services are now operational, with 
the premium train network having transported over 9.1 crore 
passengers cumulatively. Vande Bharat Sleeper services were 
launched in January 2026, with 1.21 lakh passengers carried 
in its first three months. Plans are underway to introduce 
200 additional Vande Bharat, 100 Amrit Bharat, and 50 Namo 
Bharat rapid-rail services over the next 2–3 years.
Kavach — Automatic Train Protection
The indigenously developed Kavach ATP system is 
now commissioned over 3,100 route kilometres, with 
implementation underway on an additional 24,400 km, 
significantly enhancing network safety.
Track & Network Expansion
New line construction budget for FY 2025–26 stood at `32,235 
crore, alongside `32,000 crore earmarked for track doubling. 
Cumulatively, 54,600 km of track renewal has been completed, 
improving speed and reliability across the network. Landmark 
connectivity milestones include the completion of the USBRL 
(Udhampur-Srinagar-Baramulla Rail Link), bringing rail to 
Kashmir for the first time, and the inauguration of the New 
Pamban Bridge.
Station Redevelopment
Under the Amrit Bharat Station Scheme, 1,337 stations have 
been selected for redevelopment, with over 100 completing 
their first phase of transformation and more than 1,000 under 
active development as of mid-2025.

manufacturers.

COMPANY OVERVIEWSTATUTORY REPORTFINANCIAL STATEMENTS
India’s energy sector entered a transformative phase in FY 
2025–26, marked by record capacity additions, structural 
shifts in the generation mix, and a decisive acceleration 
of the clean energy transition. As the world’s third-largest 
producer and consumer of electricity, India generated 1,845.9 
billion units (BU) of power during the year — with non-fossil 
sources contributing 29.2% of total generation, reflecting a 
fundamental reorientation of the country’s power base.
Total installed power generation capacity crossed 520 GW by 
January 2026, more than doubling since 2014, with a record 
52,537 MW of new capacity added in FY 2025–26 — the highest 
single-year addition in India’s history, surpassing the previous 
record of 34,054 MW set in FY 2024–25. Renewables accounted 
for 75% of this addition, underscoring the structural shift 
underway. India also successfully met an all-time peak power 
demand of 242.49 GW during the year, while reducing national 
energy shortages to a mere 0.03% — compared to 4.2% in 
2013–14 — and extending average electricity supply to 23.4 
hours in urban areas and 22.6 hours in rural areas.
Sources:
• PIB — Record 52,537 MW Capacity Addition FY 2025–26, Ministry of Power
Renewable Energy
FY 2025–26 stands as a watershed year for India’s energy 
transition. India secured the third position globally in renewable 
energy installed capacity, according to the Renewable Energy 
Statistics 2026 released by IRENA — surpassing Brazil. Total 
renewable energy capacity reached 274.68 GW by March 2026, 
with overall non-fossil fuel capacity at 283 GW. India achieved 
a record non-fossil fuel capacity addition of 55.3 GW in FY 
2025–26 — the highest annual increase ever recorded in the 
country’s energy history, and nearly double the 29.5 GW added 
in FY 2024–25.
Renewable energy’s share in India’s total electricity generation 
reached 26.2% in FY 2025–26, with non-fossil sources 
accounting for 29.2% of total generation. Total electricity 
generation for the year stood at 1,845.9 billion units (BU). In a 
landmark moment on 29 July 2025, renewables met 51.5% of 
India’s peak electricity demand in a single day — the highest 
renewable share ever achieved. Additionally, 83% of India’s 
power sector investment in 2024 went to clean energy, placing 
India among the world’s top nations in energy transition by 
investment share, as noted by the IEA.
India also achieved its Paris Agreement 2030 Nationally 
ENERGY
Determined Contribution (NDC) milestone in June 2025 — five 
years ahead of schedule — and remains firmly on track toward 
its target of 500 GW non-fossil fuel capacity by 2030.
Solar Energy
Solar energy continued to lead India’s renewable expansion 
in FY 2025–26, delivering record-breaking results across all 
categories. India crossed the landmark 150 GW cumulative 
solar capacity milestone, reaching 150.26 GW as of 31 March 
2026 — more than 53 times the installed base of 2.82 GW in 
2014. The annual solar capacity addition of 44.61 GW in FY 
2025–26 was the highest ever in a single financial year, nearly 
double the 23.83 GW added in FY 2024–25, and well above the 
government’s own target of 34 GW.
Utility-Scale Solar
110.43 GW of ground-mounted utility-scale solar capacity 
installed cumulatively, with 28.6 GW added in CY2025 alone — 
a 54.6% rise over the prior year.
Rooftop Solar
Cumulative rooftop solar capacity reached 25.73 GW, driven 
by the PM Surya Ghar Yojana scheme and strong Commercial 
& Industrial (C&I) sector adoption.
PM KUSUM Scheme
13.94 lakh solar pumps installed or solarised in FY 2025–26, 
with 7,672 MW capacity added under the scheme cumulatively 
reaching 13,111 MW. The scheme has delivered estimated 
savings of 12.59 million tonnes of CO2 and 734 million litres of 
diesel.
Distributed Renewable Energy
Distributed solar (DRE) contributed 16.3 GW (36%) of total 
solar additions in FY 2025–26, including 7.6 GW under PM 
KUSUM and 8.7 GW from rooftop installations — reflecting 
strong decentralised energy adoption.
Manufacturing Scale-Up
India’s PV module manufacturing capacity expanded 
significantly to support domestic supply chains, reducing 
import dependency and enabling higher bid competitiveness 
for solar developers.

India's Transmission & Distribution (T&D) sector continued 
to witness robust growth during FY 2025-26, supported by 
rising electricity demand, rapid renewable energy integration 
and sustained investments in power infrastructure. As the 
country advances towards its target of achieving 500 GW of 
non-fossil fuel capacity by 2030, strengthening transmission 
networks and modernizing distribution systems have become 
critical priorities. This transition is creating substantial 
opportunities across transmission towers, galvanized 
structures, conductors, substations, steel tubes and related 
infrastructure products.
India's power transmission network remains among the largest 
globally, with an inter-regional transmission capacity of 
approximately 118,740 MW, enabling seamless power transfer 
across regions and supporting the evacuation of over 110 GW 
of renewable energy capacity. To support future demand and 
renewable energy integration, the Government has outlined 
investments of nearly `9.15 lakh crore in transmission 
infrastructure by 2032 under the National Electricity Plan.
The transmission segment continued to witness significant 
capacity additions during CY2025. As part of the ongoing grid 
expansion programme and Green Energy Corridor initiatives, 
approximately 7,947 circuit kilometres (ckm) of transmission 
lines and 1,09,586 MVA of transformation capacity were added 
during the year. These additions are strengthening grid 
resilience, enhancing inter-state connectivity and facilitating 
the evacuation of renewable power from generation hubs to 
demand centres.
The Indian Power Transmission & Distribution EPC market is 
expected to remain on a strong growth trajectory. According 
to industry estimates, the market was valued at approximately 
US$ 14.68 billion in 2025 and is projected to reach US$ 35.20 
billion by 2035, expanding at a CAGR of 9.34%. Growth is being 
driven by increasing investments in transmission corridors, 
urbanization, industrial expansion, smart grid deployment and 
renewable energy infrastructure.
On the distribution side, the Government's Revamped 
Distribution Sector Scheme (RDSS) continued to accelerate 
network modernization and improve operational efficiencies 
across utilities. By December 2025, more than 3.9 crore smart 
meters had been installed across the country, enabling real-
TRANSMISSION & DISTRIBUTION SECTOR
time monitoring, improved billing efficiency and enhanced 
energy management. These initiatives have contributed to a 
significant reduction in Aggregate Technical & Commercial 
(AT&C) losses, which declined from 21.91% in FY 2020-21 
to 15.04% in FY 2024-25. The nationwide rollout of smart 
metering infrastructure, feeder segregation projects and 
distribution network strengthening programmes is expected 
to generate sustained demand for electrical equipment, 
transmission structures, poles, conductors and steel-
intensive infrastructure solutions.
Looking ahead, continued investments in transmission 
expansion, renewable energy evacuation systems, smart 
grids and distribution modernization are expected to create 
long-term growth opportunities for the sector. For Goodluck 
India Limited, with its strong presence in transmission 
towers, galvanized structures, precision engineering 
products and steel solutions, the evolving T&D landscape 
presents significant opportunities to participate in India's 
infrastructure development and energy transition journey.

US$ 37.8 billion in 2025, is projected to reach US$ 72.3 billion 
by 2034, registering a CAGR of 7.5%. Data and messaging 
services account for nearly 72% of the market, underscoring 
India's transition toward a data-centric digital economy.
Digital connectivity continues to deepen across the country, 
reinforcing India's position as one of the world's largest digital 
ecosystems. As of February 2026, the country had a total 
telephone subscriber base of 1.32 billion, including over 1.03 
billion internet subscribers. Broadband subscriptions have 
expanded significantly from 149.8 million in 2016 to 1.059 
billion in February 2026, reflecting the rapid adoption of digital 
TELECOM INDUSTRY
services across both urban and rural markets. Broadband 
connections crossed the landmark 1 billion threshold in 
November 2025, compared with just 131 million a decade 
ago. Supported by a tele-density of 92.66% and among the 
world's most affordable data tariffs, with data prices declining 
from `269 per GB in 2014 to nearly `8–10 per GB in FY26, 
India continues to drive large-scale digital inclusion and data 
consumption growth.

6G Vision & Roadmap
The Bharat 6G Vision Document, launched by PM Modi in 
March 2023, targets India as a global 6G leader by 2030, 
aligned with ITU-R M.2160 (IMT-2030), aiming to own at least 
10% of global 6G IP. Key initiatives include: (i) TTDF Scheme 
— 104 projects worth `275.88 crore approved for 6G R&D 
(July 2025); (ii) 100 5G Labs at academic institutions to build 
a 6G-ready ecosystem; (iii) Bharat 6G Alliance — industry-
academia-research collaborative with global MoUs; (iv) 6G 
Spectrum Roadmap covering short-term (2025–26), medium-
term (2027–30), and long-term (2031–35) bands. At MWC 2026, 
India reaffirmed its aim to be among the first countries to 
deploy 6G commercially.
Source: PIB — 6G Roadmap | Fiinews — India 6G & ITU | India TV News — 
6G Vision
Government Initiatives & Union Budget 2026–27
The Union Budget 2026–27 (presented February 1, 2026) 
allocated `73,990 crore (USD 8.37 billion) to DoT — 1.4% of 
the total Central Budget and 39% higher than revised FY26 
estimates. Including USOF, the total outlay reaches `80,927 
crore. BSNL received `28,473 crore as the single-largest 
beneficiary for network upgradation and rural expansion. 
Other key initiatives:
• PLI Scheme: `1,950 crore allocated for telecom & 
networking product manufacturing.
• India Semiconductor Mission 2.0 & Electronics 
Components Scheme: `40,000 crore expanded allocation 
to build domestic telecom supply chains.
• Telecommunications Act 2023: Replaced the colonial-
era 1885 Act, modernising spectrum management and 
right-of-way.
• PM-WANI: 4,09,403 public Wi-Fi hotspots operational as 
of February 2026.
• SATCOM: 3 licenses issued; USD 25 billion opportunity 
identified in telecom component manufacturing.
• India's Global Ranking: 1st in Annual Investment in 
Telecom Services, AI Scientific Publications, and ICT 
Services Exports.

Rural Connectivity & BharatNet Programme
Rural tele-density stands at 60.74% (April 2026) vs. urban at 
152.11%, with 554.41 million rural wireless subscribers. Rural 
internet users are growing at 2x the pace of urban users, 
constituting 42% of India's internet base. BharatNet, one 
of the world's largest rural broadband programmes, was 
upgraded under the Amended BharatNet Programme (August 
2023) with total budget of `1,39,579 crore to connect 2.64 lakh 
Gram Panchayats (ring-topology OFC) and 3.8 lakh additional 
villages on demand. Budget 2025–26 earmarked `22,000 
crore and mandated broadband to all rural government 
secondary schools and PHCs. Service standards: minimum 
25 Mbps FTTH speed and 98% network uptime via CNOC. As 
of January 2025, 1.99 lakh out of 6.5 lakh villages (30.4%) have 
been connected. In February 2026, an MoC was signed with 
Andhra Pradesh with `2,432 crore approved for 13,000+ Gram 
Panchayats. The BharatNet Udyami Model engages Village 
Level Entrepreneurs (VLEs) for last-mile connectivity delivery.
Source: IBEF — BharatNet  | PIB — Rural Development Budget | 
PolicyEdge — Amended BharatNet | TRAI — Subscription Data
Looking ahead, the sector is poised to benefit from emerging 
opportunities across telecom manufacturing, satellite 
communications, artificial intelligence-driven networks, and 
advanced digital infrastructure. India aims to manufacture 
mobile phones worth US$ 126 billion during FY 2025-26 and 
has identified a potential US$ 25 billion opportunity in telecom 
component manufacturing. The satellite communications 
(SATCOM) segment is also gaining momentum, supported 
by the issuance of new licences and the establishment of 
a comprehensive regulatory framework. With continued 
investments, supportive policy initiatives, and accelerating 
digital adoption, India's telecommunications sector is well 
positioned to remain a key enabler of economic growth and 
digital inclusion in the years ahead.

India is the world's third-largest consumer of oil and the fourth-
largest crude oil refiner globally. The Indian Oil and Gas Projects 
Market is projected to grow from USD 29.32 billion in 2024 to 
USD 85 billion by 2035 (CAGR: ~10.16%), with the industry's 
aggregate revenue currently standing at `37.2 trillion and 
earnings forecast to grow at 6.3% annually. India's oil demand 
is projected at 5.74 million barrels per day (bpd) in 2025 and 
5.99 million bpd in 2026 (OPEC), making India the primary 
driver of global oil demand growth. Planned investments in the 
sector by 2035 are estimated at `30,00,000–35,00,000 crore 
(USD 348–406 billion). India operates 23 refineries with a total 
capacity of 258.1 MMTPA — projected to reach 309.5 MMTPA 
by 2028 — while domestic petroleum product consumption in 
FY25 stood at 239.2 MMT. Natural gas demand is set to grow 
~60% by 2030, with the government targeting a rise in gas 
share in the energy mix from 6% to 15% by 2030.
Source: IBEF — Oil & Gas India | Invest India — Oil & Gas | IBEF — 
O&G Presentation
The LPG segment remains one of the most critical pillars of 
India's downstream energy landscape, underpinned by the 
government's clean cooking fuel mission. Under the Pradhan 
Mantri Ujjwala Yojana (PMUY), beneficiaries stood at ~10.35 
crore as of December 2025, with 25 lakh additional LPG 
connections approved for FY 2025-26 to attain full household 
saturation. A targeted subsidy of `300 per 14.2 kg cylinder (up 
to 9 refills per year) is extended to PMUY beneficiaries, driving 
average per capita consumption to ~4.85 refills per year in 
FY 2025-26. To improve subsidy targeting and eliminate 
leakages, 71% of PMUY consumers and 62% of non-PMUY 
consumers had completed biometric Aadhaar verification 
by December 2025, supported by a nationwide mobile-
based authentication drive launched in November 2025. 
Complementing LPG, public sector OMCs have commenced 
dispensing E20 petrol (20% ethanol blend) at over 17,400 
retail outlets. India achieved the landmark of 20% ethanol 
blending in petrol in 2025 — five years ahead of the original 
2030 target Further, Mandatory Compressed Biogas (CBG) 
Blending Obligation (CBO) was introduced from FY 2025-26 
at 1% of CNG/PNG consumption, scaling to 5% from FY 2028-
29, with 130+ CBG plants commissioned by November 2025 
— collectively reducing dependence on LPG and fossil gas 
imports.
Source: IBEF — Oil & Gas India | DD News — Year Ender 2025 Petroleum | 
PIB — Ethanol 20% Achievement | PIB — PMUY
OIL & GAS INDUSTRY
On the policy front, the government launched the 10th OALP 
Round in February 2025, offering 25 oil and gas blocks 
covering 1,91,986 sq. km, with the sector projected to attract 
USD 25 billion in E&P investment — significant given that ~90% 
of India's sedimentary basins remain unexplored. The Union 
Budget FY26 allocated `5,597 crore (USD 640.46 million) for 
Phase II of the Indian Strategic Petroleum Reserves (ISPRL) 
project, aimed at converting underground caverns into 
strategic petroleum storage to strengthen energy security. 
India is also targeting a USD 37 billion petrochemicals capex 
push (S&P), expected to drive one-third of global capacity 
additions by 2030. HPCL commissioned a new 9 MMTPA 
refinery at Barmer, Rajasthan in FY26. On EV integration, 
8,932 EV charging stations were installed at retail fuel outlets 
under FAME-II, with OMCs establishing 18,500+ additional 
charging points. Oil PSUs spent `1.07 lakh crore — 81% of 
the FY26 capex target of `1.32 lakh crore — by January 2026, 
reflecting aggressive investment momentum.
Source: IBEF — Oil & Gas India  | Business Standard — PSU Capex FY26 | 
IBEF Infographic
The Ministry of Petroleum and Natural Gas was allocated 
`30,443 crore (USD 3.2 billion) in the Union Budget 2026–27. 
India's Viksit Bharat 2047 roadmap sets ambitious targets of 
100 MMT domestic crude production, 100 BCM natural gas 
output, and 450 MMTPA refining capacity. OMCs are expected 
to post a strong profitability rebound in FY26, driven by higher 
marketing margins, softer crude prices, and improved cash 
flows. Industrial clusters have locked in 4.2 BCM of annual gas 
supply through 20-year LNG offtake deals, ensuring pipeline 
utilisation stability. With approximately 90% of sedimentary 
basins still unexplored, substantial upstream growth potential 
remains. The sector is progressively pivoting toward digital 
oilfields, clean fuel integration, and supply chain self-reliance 
under the Aatmanirbhar Bharat framework, positioning India 
as an increasingly prominent player in the global energy 
transition.

The global steel pipe market is on a steady growth path. According to Future Market Insights, the market was valued at USD 105.6 
billion in 2025 and is projected to reach USD 109.7 billion in 2026, growing further to USD 160.9 billion by 2036 at a CAGR of 3.9%. This 
translates to an absolute opportunity of USD 51.14 billion over the decade. Steel pipes are used across a wide range of industries — 
oil and gas, construction, energy, refineries, automotive, water treatment, and chemicals — making them a foundational material 
in global industrial and infrastructure development.
GLOBAL STEEL PIPES & TUBES SECTOR
Among material types, Carbon Steel is the clear leader, holding a 78% share in 2026. It is widely preferred for its strength, cost-
effectiveness, and versatility across applications from pipelines to structural frameworks. By end-use, the Automotive sector 
leads with a 14% share, driven by demand for exhaust systems and emission control components. In terms of manufacturing, pipes 
account for the largest share at 44.6% of production, with seamless pipes commanding a premium in high-pressure oil, gas, and 
power plant applications, and welded pipes serving structural and large-diameter pipeline needs.

Looking ahead, the sector is expected to benefit from rising global infrastructure investment, growing energy transition activity 
— including emerging demand for hydrogen transport pipelines — and increasingly stringent safety and environmental standards 
that are pushing buyers toward higher-grade, certified products. Key global players operating in this space include ArcelorMittal, 
Nippon Steel, Tenaris, Vallourec, United States Steel, Jindal Saw, TMK Group, SeAH Steel, and Tata Steel. The market is gradually 
shifting from a commodity-driven model toward one where product quality, specification compliance, and supply chain reliability 
are becoming key competitive differentiators.

Geographically, the United States is the fastest-growing market, expected to expand at 4.2% CAGR through 2036. This is driven 
by large-scale infrastructure spending on pipeline replacement, industrial reshoring under the Inflation Reduction Act, and trade 
policies that support domestic producers. South Korea (3.8% CAGR) benefits from strong shipbuilding and petrochemical demand, 
while the European Union (3.7% CAGR) is seeing increased procurement linked to energy transition projects and evolving carbon 
border regulations. Japan (3.6%) and the UK (3.5%) show steady growth supported by aging pipeline replacement and offshore 
wind energy construction, respectively.

INDIAN STEEL PIPES & TUBES SECTOR
The Indian steel pipes and tubes industry continued to 
demonstrate robust growth during FY 2025-26, supported 
by sustained investments in infrastructure, energy, water 
distribution networks, urban development, and industrial 
expansion. The sector remains a critical component of India's 
manufacturing and infrastructure ecosystem, catering to 
key end-user industries such as oil & gas, construction, 
automotive, power, chemicals, and irrigation.
The India steel pipes and tubes market is estimated to be worth 
USD 18.06 billion and is expected to register a CAGR of 5.4% 
during the forecast period (2025-2031). The growth is driven 
by increasing investments in pipeline infrastructure, city gas 
distribution networks, water supply projects, and industrial 
manufacturing. Demand for both seamless and welded pipes 
continues to rise, particularly in applications requiring high 
strength, durability, and corrosion resistance. The seamless 
pipes segment is projected to witness a CAGR of around 7.4% 
during 2025-31, supported by growing requirements from the 
oil & gas, power, and engineering sectors.
India's domestic consumption of steel pipes stood at 
approximately 12 million tonnes, of which 6 million tonnes 
were met through imports while 5 million tonnes were 
produced domestically. In 2025, imports accounted for 61% of 
total consumption in India, with China being the major source 
country, accounting for more than 51% of import share, 
followed by South Korea and Japan, which together account 
for 10-16%. Demand for seamless pipe has grown significantly 
over the past few years owing to its superior performance 
characteristics compared with welded pipe.
https://www.6wresearch.com/industry-report/india-steel-pipes-
tubes-market-outlook
https://economictimes.indiatimes.com/industry/indl-goods/svs/
steel/how-indias-steel-market-is-shifting-from-raw-steel-to-
customised-products/articleshow/124786469.cms
The market is influenced by several drivers that have led 
to its substantial growth. One of the primary drivers is the 
expansion of the oil and gas industry in the country: steel 
pipes and tubes are extensively used for the transportation 
of oil, gas, and other fluids in the energy sector. Increasing 
infrastructure development, including the construction of 
pipelines, refineries, and petrochemical plants, has further 
driven demand. The growth of the automotive industry and 
rising construction activity in India have also contributed to 
the increased use of steel pipes in various applications. 
Government-led infrastructure programmes such as the 
Jal Jeevan Mission, PM Gati Shakti, expansion of natural 
gas pipeline networks, railway modernisation, and urban 
infrastructure projects continue to generate substantial 
demand for steel pipes and tubes. Rising investments in 
oil and gas exploration, water transportation systems, and 
industrial facilities are further supporting market expansion.
The sector also received a significant policy boost through 
the revised Domestically Manufactured Iron and Steel 
Products (DMI&SP) Policy, which strengthens the 'Melt and 
Pour' requirement for steel used in government procurement 
projects. The policy is expected to encourage domestic 
sourcing, reduce import dependence, and create long-term 
demand opportunities for Indian steel manufacturers and 
downstream pipe producers.
https://www.spglobal.com/energy/en/news-research/latest-news/
metals/121925-india-reiterates-iron-and-steel-products-policy-
sharpens-domestic-sourcing-norms
The India steel pipes and tubes market faces several 
challenges that impact its growth and competitiveness. A 
key challenge is the availability of cheaper alternatives such 
as PVC and plastic pipes, especially in low-cost housing 
and non-industrial applications, which compete with steel 
pipes in price-sensitive segments. The fluctuating cost of 
raw materials, particularly steel, can influence pricing and 
affect manufacturer profitability. Inadequate infrastructure 
and logistics in some regions can lead to supply-chain 
delays, affecting timely delivery. The market also faces 
competition from imports, particularly from countries 
with low-cost production capabilities. Developing cost-
effective manufacturing processes, investing in advanced 
technologies, and focusing on product quality and innovation 
will be crucial in overcoming these challenges and sustaining 
growth.
Outlook
The focus on infrastructure development and the government's 
continued push for self-reliance and domestic manufacturing 
are expected to fuel growth in the steel pipes and tubes market 
in India going forward. With consistent demand from oil & gas, 
construction, automotive, and water-management end-uses, 
and with seamless pipe demand continuing to outpace welded 
pipe on the back of its superior performance characteristics, 
the sector is expected to sustain its growth trajectory through 
the 2025-2031 forecast period at the projected CAGR of 5.4%.

COMPANY OVERVIEW
ABOUT THE COMPANY
Incorporated in 1986, Goodluck India Limited (“Goodluck” or “the Company”) has grown over four decades into one of India's 
established manufacturers and exporters of precision-engineered steel products. The Company has evolved from a conventional 
steel products manufacturer into a diversified engineering solutions provider, with a deliberate strategic focus on high-value, 
technology-driven, and application-specific businesses spanning infrastructure, renewable energy, defence, railways, oil & gas, 
automotive, and precision engineering.
Goodluck operates seven manufacturing plants across Uttar Pradesh and Gujarat, including a dedicated defence manufacturing 
facility under its subsidiary, with a total installed capacity of 5,00,000 MTPA. The Company serves a base of over 600 customers 
across more than 100 countries, supported by a workforce of over 4,000 employees and certifications including ISO 9001:2008, 
IATF 16949:2016, and EN 9100:2018/AS 9100D.
PRODUCTS AND BUSINESS SEGMENTS
The Company's business portfolio is organised across four core manufacturing segments — Engineering Structures & Precision 
Fabrication, Forging Products, Precision Pipes & Auto Tubes, and CR Coils, Pipes & Tubes — complemented by an emerging fifth 
pillar in defence and aerospace, housed under a dedicated subsidiary. Together, these segments give the Company a diversified, 
application-specific product portfolio addressing high-growth sectors such as infrastructure, automotive, renewable energy, 
railways, oil & gas, and defence.
